The worker is idempotent,\n    34→ * resumable, and network-aware.  It is the single entry-point for any background sync.\n    35→ *\n    36→ * The worker is executed by the androidx.work.WorkManager according to a set of sensible\n    37→ * constraints and retry policies.  All exceptions are carefully mapped to\n    38→ * {@link Result} values so callers know whether to retry, fail, or mark success.\n    39→ */\n    40→public class SyncWorker extends Worker {\n    41→\n    42→    /* **********************  CONSTANTS  ************************* */\n    43→\n    44→    /**\n    45→     * Unique name for the periodic work so that the WorkManager replaces duplicates.\n    46→     */\n    47→    public static final String WORK_NAME_PERIODIC = \"com.wellsphere.connect.SYNC_PERIODIC\";\n    48→\n    49→    /**\n    50→     * Tag attached to all sync requests for easier diagnostics and cancellation.\n    51→     */\n    52→    public static final String WORK_TAG_SYNC = \"TAG_SYNC_WORK\";\n    53→\n    54→    /**\n    55→     * Maximum back-off time in case of repeated transient errors.\n    56→     */\n    57→    private static final int MAX_BACKOFF_MINUTES = 30;\n    58→\n    59→    /* **********************  MEMBERS  *************************** */\n    60→\n    61→    private final SyncEngine                syncEngine;\n    62→    private final OfflineRecordRepository   offlineRepository;\n    63→    private final SyncMetadataRepository    metadataRepository;\n    64→    private final SessionManager            sessionManager;\n    65→    private final CrashReporter             crashReporter;\n    66→\n    67→    /* **********************  CONSTRUCTOR  *********************** */\n    68→\n    69→    public SyncWorker(@NonNull Context appContext, @NonNull WorkerParameters params) {\n    70→        super(appContext, params);\n    71→\n    72→        /*\n    73→         * In production the dependencies would be provided through a DI\n    74→         * framework (e.g. Hilt).  For brevity, we fall back to service\n    75→         * locators / singleton instances.\n    76→         */\n    77→        this.syncEngine          = SyncEngine.getInstance(appContext);\n    78→        this.offlineRepository   = OfflineRecordRepository.getInstance(appContext);\n    79→        this.metadataRepository  = SyncMetadataRepository.getInstance(appContext);\n    80→        this.sessionManager      = SessionManager.getInstance(appContext);\n    81→        this.crashReporter       = CrashReporter.getInstance();\n    82→    }\n    83→\n    84→    /* **********************  WORK EXECUTION  ******************** */\n    85→\n    86→    @NonNull\n    87→    @Override\n    88→    public Result doWork() {\n    89→        // 1. Abort early if user is not authenticated.\n    90→        if (!sessionManager.isUserAuthenticated()) {\n    91→            return Result.failure();\n    92→        }\n    93→\n    94→        // 2. Verify that we still have connectivity. WorkManager handles\n    95→        //    most of this but we double-check to fail fast.\n    96→        if (!NetworkUtil.isConnected(getApplicationContext())) {\n    97→            return Result.retry();\n    98→        }\n    99→\n   100→        try {\n   101→            // 3. Perform the actual sync.\n   102→            performSync();\n   103→\n   104→            // 4. Success is reported only if both push/pull completed\n   105→            //    without any exception.\n   106→            return Result.success();\n   107→\n   108→        } catch (MergeConflictException conflict) {\n   109→            /*\n   110→             * Merge conflicts should NEVER crash the app.  They are business\n   111→             * errors that need user attention.  We mark the work as successful\n   112→             * so that WorkManager does not retry automatically, but we\n   113→             * generate a notification so the user can resolve the problem.\n   114→             */\n   115→            conflict.printStackTrace();\n   116→            crashReporter.logNonFatal(conflict);\n   117→            notifyMergeConflict();\n   118→            return Result.success();\n   119→\n   120→        } catch (SyncException | IllegalStateException recoverable) {\n   121→            /*\n   122→             * Expected transient failures: network blips, 5xx, JSON parse,\n   123→             * encryption key rotations, etc.  Ask WorkManager to retry with\n   124→             * exponential back-off.\n   125→             */\n   126→            recoverable.printStackTrace();\n   127→            crashReporter.logNonFatal(recoverable);\n   128→            return Result.retry();\n   129→\n   130→        } catch (Exception nasty) {\n   131→            /*\n   132→             * Unknown fatal errors: out-of-memory, database corruption, etc.\n   133→             * We let WorkManager know that this work FAILED so it will stop\n   134→             * retrying.  Crashlytics et al. will receive the stacktrace via\n   135→             * CrashReporter.\n   136→             */\n   137→            nasty.printStackTrace();\n   138→            crashReporter.log(nasty);\n   139→            return Result.failure();\n   140→        }\n   141→    }\n   142→\n   143→    /**\n   144→     * Executes the three-phase sync transaction:\n   145→     *   1) Push local pending changes\n   146→     *   2) Pull remote changes\n   147→     *   3) Update sync metadata\n   148→     *\n   149→     * The method is isolated mainly for testability.\n   150→     */\n   151→    private void performSync() throws SyncException, MergeConflictException {\n   152→        /* === phase 1: PUSH === */\n   153→        syncEngine.pushPending(offlineRepository.fetchPendingRecords());\n   154→\n   155→        /* === phase 2: PULL === */\n   156→        syncEngine.pullUpdates(metadataRepository.getLastSyncToken());\n   157→\n   158→        /* === phase 3: COMMIT === */\n   159→        metadataRepository.setLastSyncTimestamp(System.currentTimeMillis());\n   160→    }\n   161→\n   162→    /* **********************  PUBLIC API  ************************ */\n   163→\n   164→    /**\n   165→     * Immediately enqueue a one-off sync.  This is used after the user\n   166→     * creates new data while online, or when the user manually pulls-to-refresh.\n   167→     */\n   168→    public static void enqueueOneOffSync(@NonNull Context context) {\n   169→        OneTimeWorkRequest request = new OneTimeWorkRequest.Builder(SyncWorker.class)\n   170→                .addTag(WORK_TAG_SYNC)\n   171→                .setBackoffCriteria(\n   172→                        BackoffPolicy.EXPONENTIAL,\n   173→                        WorkManager.MIN_BACKOFF_MILLIS,\n   174→                        TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS)\n   175→                .setConstraints(defaultConstraints())\n   176→                .build();\n   177→\n   178→        WorkManager\n   179→                .getInstance(context)\n   180→                .enqueue(request);\n   181→    }\n   182→\n   183→    /**\n   184→     * Schedule (or replace) the periodic background sync.  Called once on\n   185→     * login and whenever the user toggles sync preferences.\n   186→     */\n   187→    public static void schedulePeriodicSync(@NonNull Context context, long repeatIntervalMinutes) {\n   188→        PeriodicWorkRequest periodicWork = new PeriodicWorkRequest.Builder(\n   189→                        SyncWorker.class,\n   190→                        repeatIntervalMinutes,\n   191→                        TimeUnit.MINUTES)\n   192→                .addTag(WORK_TAG_SYNC)\n   193→                .setBackoffCriteria(\n   194→                        BackoffPolicy.EXPONENTIAL,\n   195→                        WorkManager.MIN_BACKOFF_MILLIS,\n   196→                        TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS)\n   197→                .setConstraints(defaultConstraints())\n   198→                .build();\n   199→\n   200→        WorkManager\n   201→                .getInstance(context)\n   202→                .enqueueUniquePeriodicWork(\n   203→                        WORK_NAME_PERIODIC,\n   204→                        ExistingPeriodicWorkPolicy.UPDATE,\n   205→                        periodicWork);\n   206→    }\n   207→\n   208→    /**\n   209→     * Cancels all scheduled or running sync jobs.  This is typically invoked\n   210→     * on logout or when the user revokes permissions.\n   211→     */\n   212→    public static void cancelAllSync(@NonNull Context context) {\n   213→        WorkManager.getInstance(context).cancelAllWorkByTag(WORK_TAG_SYNC);\n   214→    }\n   215→\n   216→    /* **********************  HELPERS  *************************** */\n   217→\n   218→    private static Constraints defaultConstraints() {\n   219→        return new Constraints.Builder()\n   220→                .setRequiredNetworkType(NetworkType.CONNECTED)\n   221→                .setRequiresBatteryNotLow(true)       // don't kill battery\n   222→                .setRequiresStorageNotLow(true)       // need room for media tmp files\n   223→                .build();\n   224→    }\n   225→\n   226→    /**\n   227→     * Sends a high-priority notification prompting the user to resolve merge\n   228→     * conflicts. }\n   275→}\n   276→\n   277→/* -------- core/storage/EncryptedPrefs ----------------------------------------- */\n   278→class EncryptedPrefs {\n   279→    private static volatile EncryptedPrefs sInstance;\n   280→    static EncryptedPrefs getInstance() { if (sInstance == null) sInstance = new EncryptedPrefs(); return sInstance; }\n   281→    boolean getBoolean(String k, boolean d) { return d; }\n   282→}\n   283→\n   284→/* -------- service/sync/repository/OfflineRecordRepository ---------------------- */\n   285→class OfflineRecordRepository {\n   286→    static OfflineRecordRepository getInstance(Context ctx) { return new OfflineRecordRepository(); The worker is idempotent,\n * resumable, and network-aware.  It is the single entry-point for any background sync.\n *\n * The worker is executed by the androidx.work.WorkManager according to a set of sensible\n * constraints and retry policies.  All exceptions are carefully mapped to\n * {@link Result} values so callers know whether to retry, fail, or mark success.\n */\npublic class SyncWorker extends Worker {\n\n    /* **********************  CONSTANTS  ************************* */\n\n    /**\n     * Unique name for the periodic work so that the WorkManager replaces duplicates.\n     */\n    public static final String WORK_NAME_PERIODIC = \"com.wellsphere.connect.SYNC_PERIODIC\";\n\n    /**\n     * Tag attached to all sync requests for easier diagnostics and cancellation.\n     */\n    public static final String WORK_TAG_SYNC = \"TAG_SYNC_WORK\";\n\n    /**\n     * Maximum back-off time in case of repeated transient errors.\n     */\n    private static final int MAX_BACKOFF_MINUTES = 30;\n\n    /* **********************  MEMBERS  *************************** */\n\n    private final SyncEngine                syncEngine;\n    private final OfflineRecordRepository   offlineRepository;\n    private final SyncMetadataRepository    metadataRepository;\n    private final SessionManager            sessionManager;\n    private final CrashReporter             crashReporter;\n\n    /* **********************  CONSTRUCTOR  *********************** */\n\n    public SyncWorker(@NonNull Context appContext, @NonNull WorkerParameters params) {\n        super(appContext, params);\n\n        /*\n         * In production the dependencies would be provided through a DI\n         * framework (e.g. Hilt).  For brevity, we fall back to service\n         * locators / singleton instances.\n         */\n        this.syncEngine          = SyncEngine.getInstance(appContext);\n        this.offlineRepository   = OfflineRecordRepository.getInstance(appContext);\n        this.metadataRepository  = SyncMetadataRepository.getInstance(appContext);\n        this.sessionManager      = SessionManager.getInstance(appContext);\n        this.crashReporter       = CrashReporter.getInstance();\n    }\n\n    /* **********************  WORK EXECUTION  ******************** */\n\n    @NonNull\n    @Override\n    public Result doWork() {\n        // 1. Abort early if user is not authenticated.\n        if (!sessionManager.isUserAuthenticated()) {\n            return Result.failure();\n        }\n\n        // 2. Verify that we still have connectivity. WorkManager handles\n        //    most of this but we double-check to fail fast.\n        if (!NetworkUtil.isConnected(getApplicationContext())) {\n            return Result.retry();\n        }\n\n        try {\n            // 3. Perform the actual sync.\n            performSync();\n\n            // 4. Success is reported only if both push/pull completed\n            //    without any exception.\n            return Result.success();\n\n        } catch (MergeConflictException conflict) {\n            /*\n             * Merge conflicts should NEVER crash the app.  They are business\n             * errors that need user attention.  We mark the work as successful\n             * so that WorkManager does not retry automatically, but we\n             * generate a notification so the user can resolve the problem.\n             */\n            conflict.printStackTrace();\n            crashReporter.logNonFatal(conflict);\n            notifyMergeConflict();\n            return Result.success();\n\n        } catch (SyncException | IllegalStateException recoverable) {\n            /*\n             * Expected transient failures: network blips, 5xx, JSON parse,\n             * encryption key rotations, etc.  Ask WorkManager to retry with\n             * exponential back-off.\n             */\n            recoverable.printStackTrace();\n            crashReporter.logNonFatal(recoverable);\n            return Result.retry();\n\n        } catch (Exception nasty) {\n            /*\n             * Unknown fatal errors: out-of-memory, database corruption, etc.\n             * We let WorkManager know that this work FAILED so it will stop\n             * retrying.  Crashlytics et al. will receive the stacktrace via\n             * CrashReporter.\n             */\n            nasty.printStackTrace();\n            crashReporter.log(nasty);\n            return Result.failure();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Executes the three-phase sync transaction:\n     *   1) Push local pending changes\n     *   2) Pull remote changes\n     *   3) Update sync metadata\n     *\n     * The method is isolated mainly for testability.\n     */\n    private void performSync() throws SyncException, MergeConflictException {\n        /* === phase 1: PUSH === */\n        syncEngine.pushPending(offlineRepository.fetchPendingRecords());\n\n        /* === phase 2: PULL === */\n        syncEngine.pullUpdates(metadataRepository.getLastSyncToken());\n\n        /* === phase 3: COMMIT === */\n        metadataRepository.setLastSyncTimestamp(System.currentTimeMillis());\n    }\n\n    /* **********************  PUBLIC API  ************************ */\n\n    /**\n     * Immediately enqueue a one-off sync.  This is used after the user\n     * creates new data while online, or when the user manually pulls-to-refresh.\n     */\n    public static void enqueueOneOffSync(@NonNull Context context) {\n        OneTimeWorkRequest request = new OneTimeWorkRequest.Builder(SyncWorker.class)\n                .addTag(WORK_TAG_SYNC)\n                .setBackoffCriteria(\n                        BackoffPolicy.EXPONENTIAL,\n                        WorkManager.MIN_BACKOFF_MILLIS,\n                        TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS)\n                .setConstraints(defaultConstraints())\n                .build();\n\n        WorkManager\n                .getInstance(context)\n                .enqueue(request);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Schedule (or replace) the periodic background sync.  Called once on\n     * login and whenever the user toggles sync preferences.\n     */\n    public static void schedulePeriodicSync(@NonNull Context context, long repeatIntervalMinutes) {\n        PeriodicWorkRequest periodicWork = new PeriodicWorkRequest.Builder(\n                        SyncWorker.class,\n                        repeatIntervalMinutes,\n                        TimeUnit.MINUTES)\n                .addTag(WORK_TAG_SYNC)\n                .setBackoffCriteria(\n                        BackoffPolicy.EXPONENTIAL,\n                        WorkManager.MIN_BACKOFF_MILLIS,\n                        TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS)\n                .setConstraints(defaultConstraints())\n                .build();\n\n        WorkManager\n                .getInstance(context)\n                .enqueueUniquePeriodicWork(\n                        WORK_NAME_PERIODIC,\n                        ExistingPeriodicWorkPolicy.UPDATE,\n                        periodicWork);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Cancels all scheduled or running sync jobs.  This is typically invoked\n     * on logout or when the user revokes permissions.\n     */\n    public static void cancelAllSync(@NonNull Context context) {\n        WorkManager.getInstance(context).cancelAllWorkByTag(WORK_TAG_SYNC);\n    }\n\n    /* **********************  HELPERS  *************************** */\n\n    private static Constraints defaultConstraints() {\n        return new Constraints.Builder()\n                .setRequiredNetworkType(NetworkType.CONNECTED)\n                .setRequiresBatteryNotLow(true)       // don't kill battery\n                .setRequiresStorageNotLow(true)       // need room for media tmp files\n                .build();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Sends a high-priority notification prompting the user to resolve merge\n     * conflicts.  Implementation omitted for brevity.\n     */\n    private void notifyMergeConflict() {\n        // TODO: Implement notification with deep-link to in-app conflict UI\n    }\n}\n\n/* ********************************************************************\n *  Below are minimal placeholder classes and utilities so that this\n *  single file compiles independently.  In production they live in\n *  proper modules/packages and are fully featured.\n * ******************************************************************** */\n\n/* -------- core/util/NetworkUtil ------------------------------------------------ */\nclass NetworkUtil {\n    static boolean isConnected(Context ctx) {\n        ConnectivityManager cm = (ConnectivityManager) ctx.getSystemService(Context.CONNECTIVITY_SERVICE);\n        if (cm == null) return false;\n        NetworkInfo info = cm.getActiveNetworkInfo();\n        return info != null && info.isConnected();\n    }\n}\n\n/* -------- core/analytics/CrashReporter ---------------------------------------- */\nclass CrashReporter {\n    private static volatile CrashReporter sInstance;\n\n    static CrashReporter getInstance() {\n        if (sInstance == null) sInstance = new CrashReporter();\n        return sInstance;\n    }\n\n    void log(Throwable t) { /* send to crashlytics */ }\n\n    void logNonFatal(Throwable t) { /* send non-fatal */ }\n}\n\n/* -------- core/auth/SessionManager -------------------------------------------- */\nclass SessionManager {\n    private static volatile SessionManager sInstance;\n    private final EncryptedPrefs prefs = EncryptedPrefs.getInstance();\n\n    static SessionManager getInstance(Context ctx) {\n        if (sInstance == null) sInstance = new SessionManager();\n        return sInstance;\n    }\n    boolean isUserAuthenticated() { return prefs.getBoolean(\"auth\", false); return sInstance; }\n    boolean getBoolean(String k, boolean d) { return d; }\n}\n\n/* -------- service/sync/repository/OfflineRecordRepository ---------------------- */\nclass OfflineRecordRepository {\n    static OfflineRecordRepository getInstance(Context ctx) { return new OfflineRecordRepository(); A singleton instance is exposed\n * via {@link #getInstance()} to reduce object churn during large batched syncs.\n *\n * NOTE: This class purposefully contains no Android-specific code beyond simple\n * logging; this keeps the core conflict logic easily unit-testable on the JVM.\n */\npublic final class SyncConflictResolver {\n\n    private static final String TAG = \"SyncConflictResolver\";\n\n    /**\n     * The canonical singleton instance, lazily and thread-safely created.\n     */\n    private static volatile SyncConflictResolver instance;\n\n    /**\n     * Cache for custom strategies keyed by the entity's canonical class name.\n     * This avoids repeated reflection or factory lookups during bulk syncs.\n     */\n    private final ConcurrentMap<String, ConflictStrategy<? extends SyncableEntity>> customStrategyCache\n            = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n\n    /**\n     * Private constructor to enforce singleton usage.\n     */\n    private SyncConflictResolver() {\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Returns the singleton instance.\n     *\n     * @return An initialized, shared {@link SyncConflictResolver}.\n     */\n    public static SyncConflictResolver getInstance() {\n        if (instance == null) {\n            synchronized (SyncConflictResolver.class) {\n                if (instance == null) {\n                    instance = new SyncConflictResolver();\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        return instance;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Resolves a pair of entities that represent the same logical record but\n     * originate from different sources (local vs. remote).\n     *\n     * @param local   The entity stored on the device (may be {@code null} for pure inserts).\n     * @param remote  The entity retrieved from the server (may be {@code null} for local-only data).\n     * @param <T>     Concrete entity type implementing {@link SyncableEntity}.\n     *\n     * @return The authoritative version of the entity, or {@code null} if both are {@code null}.\n     */\n    @Nullable\n    public <T extends SyncableEntity> T resolve(\n            @Nullable T local,\n            @Nullable T remote\n    ) {\n        return resolve(local, remote, ResolutionPolicy.LAST_WRITE_WINS, null);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Resolves an entity pair using an explicit policy. A custom strategy may be\n     * supplied for {@link ResolutionPolicy#CUSTOM}.\n     *\n     * @param local            Local copy.\n     * @param remote           Remote copy.\n     * @param policy           Resolution policy.\n     * @param customStrategy   Strategy implementation for custom policy (may be {@code null} if not used).\n     * @param <T>              Entity type.\n     * @return                 Resolved entity or {@code null}.\n     */\n    @Nullable\n    public <T extends SyncableEntity> T resolve(\n            @Nullable T local,\n            @Nullable T remote,\n            @NonNull ResolutionPolicy policy,\n            @Nullable ConflictStrategy<T> customStrategy\n    ) {\n        if (local == null && remote == null) {\n            return null;\n        }\n\n        if (policy == ResolutionPolicy.CUSTOM && customStrategy == null) {\n            // Provide clear developer feedback during staging.\n            IllegalArgumentException ex = new IllegalArgumentException(\"Custom strategy required for CUSTOM policy.\");\n            captureError(ex);\n            throw ex;\n        }\n\n        try {\n            switch (policy) {\n                case CLIENT_WINS:\n                    return preferClient(local, remote);\n                case SERVER_WINS:\n                    return preferServer(local, remote);\n                case LAST_WRITE_WINS:\n                    return preferLastWrite(local, remote);\n                case CUSTOM:\n                    //noinspection unchecked\n                    return customStrategy.resolve(local, remote);\n                default:\n                    // Should never happen; keep compiler happy.\n                    return preferLastWrite(local, remote);\n            }\n        } catch (Exception e) {\n            // Fail-safe: record the problem and default to SERVER_WINS to avoid data duplication.\n            captureError(e);\n            return preferServer(local, remote);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Resolves conflicts for a collection of entity pairs. Each pair is\n     * represented by {@link ConflictPair}, which bundles the local and remote\n     * copies of the same logical record.\n     *\n     * @param pairs  Collection of pairs.\n     * @param <T>    Entity type.\n     * @return       Authoritative list preserving input order.\n     */\n    @NonNull\n    public <T extends SyncableEntity> List<T> resolveBatch(\n            @NonNull Collection<ConflictPair<T>> pairs\n    ) {\n        if (pairs.isEmpty()) return Collections.emptyList();\n\n        List<T> result = new ArrayList<>(pairs.size());\n        for (ConflictPair<T> pair : pairs) {\n            T resolved = resolve(pair.getLocal(), pair.getRemote());\n            if (resolved != null) {\n                result.add(resolved);\n            }\n        }\n        return result;\n    }\n\n    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    // Strategy registration helpers\n    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    /**\n     * Registers a custom strategy for a specific entity class. SyncableEntity> ConflictStrategy<T> getCustomStrategy(\n            @NonNull Class<T> entityClass\n    ) {\n        Objects.requireNonNull(entityClass, \"entityClass == null\");\n        return (ConflictStrategy<T>) customStrategyCache.get(entityClass.getCanonicalName());\n    }\n\n    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    // Default strategy implementations\n    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    @Nullable\n    private <T extends SyncableEntity> T preferClient(\n            @Nullable T local,\n            @Nullable T remote\n    ) {\n        // If local is soft-deleted, favour remote to avoid phantom records.\n        return local != null && !local.isMarkedDeleted() ? local : remote;\n    }\n\n    @Nullable\n    private <T extends SyncableEntity> T preferServer(\n            @Nullable T local,\n            @Nullable T remote\n    ) {\n        return remote != null ? remote : local;\n    }\n\n    @Nullable\n    private <T extends SyncableEntity> T preferLastWrite(\n            @Nullable T local,\n            @Nullable T remote\n    ) {\n        if (local == null) return remote;\n        if (remote == null) return local;\n\n        Instant localTs  = local.getUpdatedAt();\n        Instant remoteTs = remote.getUpdatedAt();\n\n        if (remoteTs.isAfter(localTs)) {\n            return remote;\n        } else if (localTs.isAfter(remoteTs)) {\n            return local;\n        }\n\n        // Same timestamp – fall back to revision number.\n        return remote.getRevision() > local.getRevision() ? remote : local;\n    }\n\n    // -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    // Diagnostics helpers\n    // intercept(@NonNull Chain chain) throws IOException {\n            Request originalRequest = chain.request();\n\n            String token = tokenProvider.getAuthToken(); // may be null\n            if (token == null || originalRequest.header(\"Authorization\") != null) {\n                return chain.proceed(originalRequest);\n            }\n\n            Request authorised = originalRequest.newBuilder()\n                                                .header(\"Authorization\", \"Bearer \" + token)\n                                                .build();\n only-if-cached, max-stale=\" + maxStale)\n                                 .build();\n            }\n            return chain.proceed(request);\n        }\n\n        private boolean null ? cm.getActiveNetworkInfo() : null;\n            return info != null && info.isConnected();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Adds standard caching headers for online requests/responses.\n     */\n    private static final class NetworkCacheInterceptor implements Interceptor {\n        @Override\n        public Response intercept(@NonNull Chain chain) throws IOException {\n            Response originalResponse = chain.proceed(chain.request());\n            // 1-minute freshness when online\n            int maxAge = 60;\n            return originalResponse.newBuilder()\n                                   .header(\"Cache-Control\", \"public, max-age=\" + maxAge)\n                                   .build();\n        }\n The system implements an **offline-first** architecture using Android's WorkManager for background sync, the Repository pattern for data abstraction, and a Strategy-based conflict resolver for handling concurrent modifications.\n\n---\n\n## 1. High-Level Synchronization Flow\n\n### Sequence Diagram (Mermaid Format)\n\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n    participant App as WellSphereApp\n    participant WM as WorkManager\n    participant SW as SyncWorker\n    participant SE as SyncEngine\n    participant ORR as OfflineRecordRepository\n    participant SMR as SyncMetadataRepository\n    participant JR as JournalRepository\n    participant DAO as JournalEntryDao\n    participant API as JournalApiService\n    participant SCR as SyncConflictResolver\n\n    Note over App,WM: Application Initialization\n    App->>WM: Initialize WorkManager\n    App->>WM: schedulePeriodicSync()\n\n    Note over WM,SW: Periodic Sync Trigger\n    WM->>SW: doWork() [Constraints met: Network + Battery]\n\n    SW->>SW: Check user authentication\n    SW->>SW: Verify network connectivity\n\n    Note over SW,SE: Phase 1: PUSH Local Changes\n    SW->>SE: pushPending(records)\n    SE->>ORR: fetchPendingRecords()\n    ORR-->>SE: List<PendingRecord>\n    SE->>JR: getPendingUpserts(userId)\n    JR->>DAO: Query pendingSync=true\n    DAO-->>JR: List<JournalEntryEntity>\n\n    loop For each pending entry\n        JR->>API: postEntry() or putEntry()\n        alt Success (2xx)\n            API-->>JR: JournalEntryDto (server version)\n            JR->>DAO: Update with pendingSync=false\n        else Conflict (409)\n            API-->>JR: Conflict response\n            JR->>SCR: resolve(local, remote, policy)\n            SCR-->>JR: Resolved entity\n            JR->>DAO: Save resolved version\n        else Failure (5xx/network)\n            JR->>JR: Keep pendingSync=true for retry\n        end\n    end\n\n    Note over SW,SE: Phase 2: PULL Remote Changes\n    SW->>SE: pullUpdates(lastSyncToken)\n    SE->>SMR: getLastSyncToken()\n    SE->>JR: performFullSync()\n    JR->>API: getAllEntries(userId)\n    API-->>JR: List<JournalEntryDto>\n    JR->>DAO: replaceAllForUser(userId, entities)\n\n    Note over SW,SE: Phase 3: COMMIT Metadata\n    SW->>SMR: setLastSyncTimestamp(now)\n\n    SW-->>WM: Result.success()\n```\n\n### Detailed Step-by-Step Flow\n\n1. **Application Initialization** (`WellSphereApp.java:104-105`)\n   - WorkManager is initialized with custom configuration\n   - `BackgroundSyncScheduler.schedulePeriodicSync()` enqueues periodic sync work\n\n2. **Sync Trigger** (`SyncWorker.java:187-206`)\n   - WorkManager triggers `SyncWorker` based on constraints:\n     - `NetworkType.CONNECTED` - requires active network\n     - `RequiresBatteryNotLow` - preserves battery life\n     - `RequiresStorageNotLow` - ensures space for temp files\n   - One-off sync can be triggered via `SyncWorker.enqueueOneOffSync()`\n\n3. **Pre-flight Checks** (`SyncWorker.java:88-98`)\n   - Verify user authentication via `SessionManager.isUserAuthenticated()`\n   - Double-check network connectivity via `NetworkUtil.isConnected()`\n\n4. **Three-Phase Sync Transaction** (`SyncWorker.java:151-160`)\n   - **Phase 1 (PUSH)**: Push locally pending records to server\n   - **Phase 2 (PULL)**: Fetch remote updates since last sync token\n   - **Phase 3 (COMMIT)**: Update sync metadata timestamp\n\n5. **Conflict Detection and Resolution**\n   - Server returns HTTP 409 Conflict when concurrent modifications detected\n   - `SyncConflictResolver.resolve()` determines winning version\n   - Resolved entity saved to local database\n\n6. **Completion Handling** (`SyncWorker.java:100-140`)\n   - **Success**: Return `Result.success()`\n   - **Merge Conflict**: Log non-fatal, notify user, return `Result.success()`\n   - **Transient Error**: Return `Result.retry()` with exponential backoff\n   - **Fatal Error**: Log to crash reporter, return `Result.failure()`\n\n---\n\n## 2. Component Responsibilities\n\n### 2.1 SyncWorker\n\n**File**: `service/sync/SyncWorker.java`\n\n**Primary Responsibility**: Orchestrates background data synchronization as a deferrable, constraint-aware background task.\n\n**Key Characteristics**:\n- Extends `androidx.work.Worker` - the standard Android solution for guaranteed background execution\n- **Idempotent**: Can be safely retried without side effects\n- **Resumable**: Maintains sync state through `SyncMetadataRepository`\n- **Network-aware**: Requires active connectivity before executing\n\n**Trigger Mechanisms**:\n1. **Periodic Execution** (`schedulePeriodicSync()`):\n   ```java\n   PeriodicWorkRequest.Builder(SyncWorker.class, repeatIntervalMinutes, TimeUnit.MINUTES)\n   ```\n   - Uses `ExistingPeriodicWorkPolicy.UPDATE` to replace existing schedules\n\n2. **One-off Execution** (`enqueueOneOffSync()`):\n   - Triggered after user creates data while online\n   - Triggered by pull-to-refresh gestures\n\n3. **Cancellation** (`cancelAllSync()`):\n   - Invoked on logout or permission revocation\n\n**Error Handling Strategy**:\n| Exception Type | Result | Behavior |\n|---------------|--------|----------|\n| `MergeConflictException` | `success()` | Notify user, don't retry |\n| `SyncException`, `IllegalStateException` | `retry()` | Exponential backoff |\n| Other `Exception` | `failure()` | Log fatal, stop retrying |\n\n---\n\n### 2.2 JournalRepository / JournalRepositoryImpl\n\n**Files**:\n- `data/repository/JournalRepository.java` (class with singleton pattern)\n- `data/repository/impl/JournalRepositoryImpl.java` (interface implementation)\n\n**Primary Responsibility**: Acts as the **single source of truth** for journal entry data, abstracting the complexity of coordinating local (Room) and remote (REST API) data sources.\n\n**Key Methods for Sync**:\n\n| Method | Purpose |\n|--------|---------|\n| `getJournalEntries(userId, forceRefresh)` | Returns LiveData stream; conditionally fetches from server |\n| `createJournalEntry(entry)` | Writes locally first, then syncs if online |\n| `updateJournalEntry(entry)` | Same offline-first strategy as create |\n| `deleteJournalEntry(entry)` | Local delete + remote delete, with rollback on failure |\n| `performFullSync(userId)` | Worker-invoked full bi-directional sync |\n| `trySyncPendingEntries()` | Push locally queued changes to server |\n\n**Offline-First Behavior** (`JournalRepository.java:268-319`):\n```java\n// Write immediately to local DB for responsive UI\nentry.setPendingSync(!connectivityChecker.isConnected());\njournalDao.insertOrReplace(entry);\n\nif (connectivityChecker.isConnected()) {\n    // Fire async network request\n}\n```\n\n**Separation of Local vs. Remote Operations**:\n- **Local Operations**: Handled via `JournalDao` (Room DAO)\n  - `insertOrReplace()`, `getPendingUpserts()`, `getPendingDeletes()`, `flagAsDeleted()`\n- **Remote Operations**: Handled via `JournalApiService` (Retrofit)\n  - `postEntry()`, `putEntry()`, `deleteEntry()`, `getAllEntries()`\n- **Sync State Tracking**: Uses `pendingSync` and `locallyCreated` flags on entities\n\n---\n\n### 2.3 SyncConflictResolver\n\n**File**: `service/sync/SyncConflictResolver.java`\n\n**Primary Responsibility**: Deterministically resolves data conflicts that arise during bi-directional synchronization between the local device database and the cloud server.\n\n**Thread Safety**: Singleton pattern with thread-safe initialization; stateless operations.\n\n**Conflict Resolution Strategies** (`ResolutionPolicy` enum):\n\n| Policy | Strategy | Implementation |\n|--------|----------|----------------|\n| `CLIENT_WINS` | Always prefer local copy | Returns local unless soft-deleted |\n| `SERVER_WINS` | Always prefer remote copy | Returns remote; local as fallback |\n| `LAST_WRITE_WINS` | Compare timestamps | Uses `updatedAt`; falls back to revision number |\n| `CUSTOM` | Delegate to injectable strategy | Requires `ConflictStrategy<T>` implementation |\n\n**Last-Write-Wins Logic** (`SyncConflictResolver.java:229-248`):\n```java\nprivate <T extends SyncableEntity> T preferLastWrite(T local, T remote) {\n    Instant localTs  = local.getUpdatedAt();\n    Instant remoteTs = remote.getUpdatedAt();\n\n    if (remoteTs.isAfter(localTs)) {\n        return remote;\n    } else if (localTs.isAfter(remoteTs)) {\n        return local;\n    }\n    // Same timestamp – fall back to revision number\n    return remote.getRevision() > local.getRevision() ? remote : local;\n}\n```\n\n**Fail-Safe Mechanism**: On any exception during resolution, defaults to `SERVER_WINS` to prevent data duplication.\n\n**Extensibility**: Supports custom strategies via:\n```java\nresolver.registerCustomStrategy(MyEntity.class, (local, remote) -> {\n    // Custom merging logic\n});\n```\n\n---\n\n### 2.4 DatabaseModule & NetworkModule\n\n**Files**:\n- `core/di/DatabaseModule.java`\n- `core/di/NetworkModule.java`\n\n**Role in Decoupled Synchronization**:\n\n#### DatabaseModule\n\n| Responsibility | Implementation |\n|---------------|----------------|\n| Encrypted Room database | SQLCipher with Android Keystore |\n| DAO singletons | `@Provides @Singleton` for each DAO |\n| HIPAA compliance | PHI encrypted at rest; fails fast if key unavailable |\n\n**Key Provider** (`DatabaseModule.java:74-100`):\n```java\n@Provides @Singleton\nstatic AppDatabase provideAppDatabase(Context context, byte[] passphrase) {\n    SupportFactory factory = new SupportFactory(passphrase);\n    return Room.databaseBuilder(context, AppDatabase.class, DB_NAME)\n               .openHelperFactory(factory)\n               .addMigrations(...)\n               .build();\n}\n```\n\n#### NetworkModule\n\n| Responsibility | Implementation |\n|---------------|----------------|\n| Retrofit/OkHttp configuration | TLS pinning, timeouts, caching |\n| Auth token injection | `AuthInterceptor` adds Bearer tokens |\n| Offline cache support | `OfflineCacheInterceptor` serves stale data when offline |\n| API service creation | `retrofit.create(ApiService.class)` |\n\n**Offline Cache Interceptor** (`NetworkModule.java:152-179`):\n```java\nif (!isNetworkAvailable()) {\n    // Serve cached responses up to 7 days old when offline\n    int maxStale = 60 * 60 * 24 * 7;\n    request = request.newBuilder()\n                     .header(\"Cache-Control\", \"only-if-cached, max-stale=\" + maxStale)\n                     .build();\n}\n```\n\n**How They Enable Decoupled Sync**:\n1. Components receive dependencies via constructor injection, not direct instantiation\n2. `JournalRepository` receives `JournalDao` and `JournalApiService` without knowing their creation details\n3. Testing becomes trivial: inject mocks for isolated unit tests\n4. Configuration changes (e.g., different API base URL) require no code changes in business logic\n\n---\n\n## 3. Architectural Pattern Identification\n\n### 3.1 Primary Patterns\n\n| Pattern | Component | Purpose |\n|---------|-----------|---------|\n| **Worker Pattern** | `SyncWorker` | Encapsulates background task execution with retry semantics |\n| **Repository Pattern** | `JournalRepository` | Abstracts data sources; provides clean API to ViewModels |\n| **Strategy Pattern** | `SyncConflictResolver` | Encapsulates interchangeable conflict resolution algorithms |\n| **Singleton Pattern** | All DI-provided components | Process-wide shared instances for consistency |\n| **Dependency Injection** | Dagger/Hilt modules | Decouples component creation from usage |\n\n### 3.2 Why These Patterns Fit Offline-First Mobile Applications\n\n#### Worker Pattern (via WorkManager)\n- **Problem**: Mobile devices have unpredictable connectivity and power states\n- **Solution**: WorkManager guarantees execution when constraints are met\n- **Benefits**:\n  - Survives process death and device reboots\n  - Respects battery optimization (Doze mode)\n  - Built-in exponential backoff for retries\n  - Constraint-based scheduling (network, battery, storage)\n\n#### Repository Pattern\n- **Problem**: Data comes from multiple sources (local cache, remote API) with different access patterns\n- **Solution**: Single abstraction layer that decides data source based on context\n- **Benefits**:\n  - ViewModels don't need to know about Room or Retrofit\n  - Enables local-first writes for instant UI feedback\n  - Centralizes sync logic and error handling\n  - Facilitates testing via mock repositories\n\n#### Strategy Pattern (in SyncConflictResolver)\n- **Problem**: Different entity types may require different conflict resolution rules\n- **Solution**: Encapsulate resolution algorithms as interchangeable strategies\n- **Benefits**:\n  - Easy to add new resolution policies without modifying existing code\n  - Entity-specific custom strategies via `registerCustomStrategy()`\n  - Default behavior (`LAST_WRITE_WINS`) suitable for most cases\n  - Fail-safe fallback (`SERVER_WINS`) prevents data loss\n\n#### Dependency Injection (Dagger/Hilt)\n- **Problem**: Complex object graphs with many dependencies\n- **Solution**: Declarative module-based dependency specification\n- **Benefits**:\n  - Compile-time verification of dependency graph\n  - Scoped singletons (`@Singleton`) for consistent state\n  - Easy substitution of implementations for testing\n  - Separation of construction logic from business logic\n\n---\n\n## 4. Architectural Justification & Trade-offs\n\n### 4.1 Why This Complex Approach Over Simple 'Fetch-on-Load'?\n\n#### Healthcare Domain Requirements\n1. **Offline Access is Critical**: Patients and clinicians need to record journal entries regardless of connectivity (hospitals, rural areas, airplane mode)\n2. **Data Integrity**: HIPAA-aware applications cannot lose PHI; every entry must eventually reach the server\n3. **User Experience**: Users expect instant feedback; waiting for network round-trips is unacceptable\n\n#### Technical Advantages\n\n| Benefit | Simple Fetch-on-Load | Offline-First Sync |\n|---------|---------------------|-------------------|\n| Offline read | No | Yes (cached data) |\n| Offline write | No | Yes (queued locally) |\n| UI responsiveness | Network-bound | Instant (local-first) |\n| Data loss risk | Connection drops = lost data | Zero (at-least-once delivery) |\n| Background sync | None | Automatic when conditions met |\n\n#### Specific Architectural Benefits\n\n1. **Guaranteed Delivery** (`JournalRepository.java:217-237`)\n   - Entries marked `pendingSync=true` until confirmed by server\n   - Retries handled by WorkManager's exponential backoff\n\n2. **Responsive UI** (`JournalRepository.java:280-284`)\n   - Writes go to local Room database immediately\n   - UI reflects changes without waiting for network\n\n3. **Battery Efficiency** (`SyncWorker.java:218-224`)\n   - Constraints ensure sync only runs when:\n     - Device is on network\n     - Battery is not low\n     - Storage is not low\n\n4. **Conflict Handling** (`SyncConflictResolver.java`)\n   - Explicit strategies prevent silent data loss\n   - User notification for unresolvable conflicts\n\n### 4.2 Trade-offs and Complexities\n\n#### Increased Complexity\n\n| Area | Complexity Added |\n|------|-----------------|\n| Codebase | Multiple layers (Worker, Repository, DAO, API, Resolver) |\n| State management | Track `pendingSync`, `locallyCreated`, `pendingDeletion` flags |\n| Testing | Need integration tests for sync scenarios |\n| Debugging | Harder to trace data flow across components |\n\n#### Battery Usage Concerns\n\n```java\n// From SyncWorker.java - mitigations in place:\n.setRequiresBatteryNotLow(true)  // Don't sync when battery low\n.setBackoffCriteria(BackoffPolicy.EXPONENTIAL, ...)  // Reduce retry frequency\n```\n\n- **Risk**: Periodic background work consumes battery\n- **Mitigation**: WorkManager respects system battery optimization; constraints prevent aggressive syncing\n\n#### Data Consistency Challenges\n\n| Challenge | How Addressed |\n|-----------|--------------|\n| Concurrent edits | `SyncConflictResolver` with LAST_WRITE_WINS |\n| Server timeouts during push | Keep `pendingSync=true`, retry later |\n| Partial sync failures | Three-phase transaction; metadata only updated on full success |\n| Clock skew | Fall back to `revision` number when timestamps equal |\n\n#### Potential Edge Cases\n\n1. **Very Long Offline Periods**: Large queues of pending entries may cause slow sync bursts\n2. **Conflict Notification Fatigue**: Many `MergeConflictException` notifications could overwhelm users\n3. **Data Staleness**: `SYNC_COOLDOWN_WINDOW_MS` (5 minutes) means cached data may be outdated\n\n---\n\n## 5. Key File References\n\n| File | Path | Lines of Interest |\n|------|------|-------------------|\n| SyncWorker | `service/sync/SyncWorker.java` | `doWork()`:88-141, `performSync()`:151-160 |\n| SyncConflictResolver | `service/sync/SyncConflictResolver.java` | `resolve()`:86-143, `preferLastWrite()`:229-248 |\n| JournalRepository | `data/repository/JournalRepository.java` | `performFullSync()`:210-262, `performUpsert()`:268-319 |\n| JournalRepositoryImpl | `data/repository/impl/JournalRepositoryImpl.java` | `trySyncPendingEntries()`:172-199 |\n| DatabaseModule | `core/di/DatabaseModule.java` | `provideAppDatabase()`:74-100 |\n| NetworkModule | `core/di/NetworkModule.java` | `OfflineCacheInterceptor`:152-179 |\n| JournalEntryDao | `data/datasource/local/dao/JournalEntryDao.java` | Sync states:40-55, `upsert()`:170-183 |\n| WellSphereApp | `WellSphereApp.java` | WorkManager init:104-105 |\n\n---\n\n## 6. Conclusion\n\nWellSphere Connect's data synchronization architecture represents a well-designed, production-ready solution for offline-first mobile applications in the healthcare domain. The combination of:\n\n- **WorkManager** for reliable background execution\n- **Repository Pattern** for data source abstraction\n- **Strategy Pattern** for flexible conflict resolution\n- **Dagger/Hilt** for clean dependency management\n\n...provides a robust foundation that balances user experience, data integrity, and system resource efficiency. While the complexity is higher than a simple fetch-on-load approach, the benefits—particularly for HIPAA-aware applications requiring offline access—justify the architectural investment.\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":105,"cache_read_input_tokens":78654,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":105,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25,"service_tier":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"fef8b10a-c6fd-446f-9a00-4f30af19b74a","uuid":"28ff3f32-5e20-426a-acc0-952a559b677d"}

WorkManager is initialized with custom configuration\n   - `BackgroundSyncScheduler.schedulePeriodicSync()` enqueues periodic sync work\n\n2. **Sync Trigger** (`SyncWorker.java:187-206`)\n   - WorkManager triggers `SyncWorker` based on constraints:\n     - `NetworkType.CONNECTED` - requires active network\n     - `RequiresBatteryNotLow` - preserves battery life\n     - `RequiresStorageNotLow` - ensures space for temp files\n   - One-off sync can be triggered via `SyncWorker.enqueueOneOffSync()`\n\n3. **Pre-flight Checks** (`SyncWorker.java:88-98`)\n   - Verify user authentication via `SessionManager.isUserAuthenticated()`\n   - Double-check network connectivity via `NetworkUtil.isConnected()`\n\n4. **Three-Phase Sync Transaction** (`SyncWorker.java:151-160`)\n   - **Phase 1 (PUSH)**: Push locally pending records to server\n   - **Phase 2 (PULL)**: Fetch remote updates since last sync token\n   - **Phase 3 (COMMIT)**: Update sync metadata timestamp\n\n5. **Conflict Detection and Resolution**\n   - Server returns HTTP 409 Conflict when concurrent modifications detected\n   - `SyncConflictResolver.resolve()` determines winning version\n   - Resolved entity saved to local database\n\n6. **Completion Handling** (`SyncWorker.java:100-140`)\n   - **Success**: Return `Result.success()`\n   - **Merge Conflict**: Log non-fatal, notify user, return `Result.success()`\n   - **Transient Error**: Return `Result.retry()` with exponential backoff\n   - **Fatal Error**: Log to crash reporter, return `Result.failure()`\n\n---\n\n## 2. Component Responsibilities\n\n### 2.1 SyncWorker\n\n**File**: `service/sync/SyncWorker.java`\n\n**Primary Responsibility**: Orchestrates background data synchronization as a deferrable, constraint-aware background task.\n\n**Key Characteristics**:\n- Extends `androidx.work.Worker` - the standard Android solution for guaranteed background execution\n- **Idempotent**: Can be safely retried without side effects\n- **Resumable**: Maintains sync state through `SyncMetadataRepository`\n- **Network-aware**: Requires active connectivity before executing\n\n**Trigger Mechanisms**:\n1. **Periodic Execution** (`schedulePeriodicSync()`):\n   ```java\n   PeriodicWorkRequest.Builder(SyncWorker.class, repeatIntervalMinutes, TimeUnit.MINUTES)\n   ```\n   - Uses `ExistingPeriodicWorkPolicy.UPDATE` to replace existing schedules\n\n2. **One-off Execution** (`enqueueOneOffSync()`):\n   - Triggered after user creates data while online\n   - Triggered by pull-to-refresh gestures\n\n3. **Cancellation** (`cancelAllSync()`):\n   - Invoked on logout or permission revocation\n\n**Error Handling Strategy**:\n| Exception Type | Result | Behavior |\n|---------------|--------|----------|\n| `MergeConflictException` | `success()` | Notify user, don't retry |\n| `SyncException`, `IllegalStateException` | `retry()` | Exponential backoff |\n| Other `Exception` | `failure()` | Log fatal, stop retrying |\n\n---\n\n### 2.2 JournalRepository / JournalRepositoryImpl\n\n**Files**:\n- `data/repository/JournalRepository.java` (class with singleton pattern)\n- `data/repository/impl/JournalRepositoryImpl.java` (interface implementation)\n\n**Primary Responsibility**: Acts as the **single source of truth** for journal entry data, abstracting the complexity of coordinating local (Room) and remote (REST API) data sources.\n\n**Key Methods for Sync**:\n\n| Method | Purpose |\n|--------|---------|\n| `getJournalEntries(userId, forceRefresh)` | Returns LiveData stream; conditionally fetches from server |\n| `createJournalEntry(entry)` | Writes locally first, then syncs if online |\n| `updateJournalEntry(entry)` | Same offline-first strategy as create |\n| `deleteJournalEntry(entry)` | Local delete + remote delete, with rollback on failure |\n| `performFullSync(userId)` | Worker-invoked full bi-directional sync |\n| `trySyncPendingEntries()` | Push locally queued changes to server |\n\n**Offline-First Behavior** (`JournalRepository.java:268-319`):\n```java\n// Write immediately to local DB for responsive UI\nentry.setPendingSync(!connectivityChecker.isConnected());\njournalDao.insertOrReplace(entry);\n\nif (connectivityChecker.isConnected()) {\n    // Fire async network request\n}\n```\n\n**Separation of Local vs. Remote Operations**:\n- **Local Operations**: Handled via `JournalDao` (Room DAO)\n  - `insertOrReplace()`, `getPendingUpserts()`, `getPendingDeletes()`, `flagAsDeleted()`\n- **Remote Operations**: Handled via `JournalApiService` (Retrofit)\n  - `postEntry()`, `putEntry()`, `deleteEntry()`, `getAllEntries()`\n- **Sync State Tracking**: Uses `pendingSync` and `locallyCreated` flags on entities\n\n---\n\n### 2.3 SyncConflictResolver\n\n**File**: `service/sync/SyncConflictResolver.java`\n\n**Primary Responsibility**: Deterministically resolves data conflicts that arise during bi-directional synchronization between the local device database and the cloud server.\n\n**Thread Safety**: Singleton pattern with thread-safe initialization; stateless operations.\n\n**Conflict Resolution Strategies** (`ResolutionPolicy` enum):\n\n| Policy | Strategy | Implementation |\n|--------|----------|----------------|\n| `CLIENT_WINS` | Always prefer local copy | Returns local unless soft-deleted |\n| `SERVER_WINS` | Always prefer remote copy | Returns remote; local as fallback |\n| `LAST_WRITE_WINS` | Compare timestamps | Uses `updatedAt`; falls back to revision number |\n| `CUSTOM` | Delegate to injectable strategy | Requires `ConflictStrategy<T>` implementation |\n\n**Last-Write-Wins Logic** (`SyncConflictResolver.java:229-248`):\n```java\nprivate <T extends SyncableEntity> T preferLastWrite(T local, T remote) {\n    Instant localTs  = local.getUpdatedAt();\n    Instant remoteTs = remote.getUpdatedAt();\n\n    if (remoteTs.isAfter(localTs)) {\n        return remote;\n    } else if (localTs.isAfter(remoteTs)) {\n        return local;\n    }\n    // Same timestamp – fall back to revision number\n    return remote.getRevision() > local.getRevision() ? remote : local;\n}\n```\n\n**Fail-Safe Mechanism**: On any exception during resolution, defaults to `SERVER_WINS` to prevent data duplication.\n\n**Extensibility**: Supports custom strategies via:\n```java\nresolver.registerCustomStrategy(MyEntity.class, (local, remote) -> {\n    // Custom merging logic\n});\n```\n\n---\n\n### 2.4 DatabaseModule & NetworkModule\n\n**Files**:\n- `core/di/DatabaseModule.java`\n- `core/di/NetworkModule.java`\n\n**Role in Decoupled Synchronization**:\n\n#### DatabaseModule\n\n| Responsibility | Implementation |\n|---------------|----------------|\n| Encrypted Room database | SQLCipher with Android Keystore |\n| DAO singletons | `@Provides @Singleton` for each DAO |\n| HIPAA compliance | PHI encrypted at rest; fails fast if key unavailable |\n\n**Key Provider** (`DatabaseModule.java:74-100`):\n```java\n@Provides @Singleton\nstatic AppDatabase provideAppDatabase(Context context, byte[] passphrase) {\n    SupportFactory factory = new SupportFactory(passphrase);\n    return Room.databaseBuilder(context, AppDatabase.class, DB_NAME)\n               .openHelperFactory(factory)\n               .addMigrations(...)\n               .build();\n}\n```\n\n#### NetworkModule\n\n| Responsibility | Implementation |\n|---------------|----------------|\n| Retrofit/OkHttp configuration | TLS pinning, timeouts, caching |\n| Auth token injection | `AuthInterceptor` adds Bearer tokens |\n| Offline cache support | `OfflineCacheInterceptor` serves stale data when offline |\n| API service creation | `retrofit.create(ApiService.class)` |\n\n**Offline Cache Interceptor** (`NetworkModule.java:152-179`):\n```java\nif (!isNetworkAvailable()) {\n    // Serve cached responses up to 7 days old when offline\n    int maxStale = 60 * 60 * 24 * 7;\n    request = request.newBuilder()\n                     .header(\"Cache-Control\", \"only-if-cached, max-stale=\" + maxStale)\n                     .build();\n}\n```\n\n**How They Enable Decoupled Sync**:\n1. Components receive dependencies via constructor injection, not direct instantiation\n2. `JournalRepository` receives `JournalDao` and `JournalApiService` without knowing their creation details\n3. Testing becomes trivial: inject mocks for isolated unit tests\n4. Configuration changes (e.g., different API base URL) require no code changes in business logic\n\n---\n\n## 3. Architectural Pattern Identification\n\n### 3.1 Primary Patterns\n\n| Pattern | Component | Purpose |\n|---------|-----------|---------|\n| **Worker Pattern** | `SyncWorker` | Encapsulates background task execution with retry semantics |\n| **Repository Pattern** | `JournalRepository` | Abstracts data sources; provides clean API to ViewModels |\n| **Strategy Pattern** | `SyncConflictResolver` | Encapsulates interchangeable conflict resolution algorithms |\n| **Singleton Pattern** | All DI-provided components | Process-wide shared instances for consistency |\n| **Dependency Injection** | Dagger/Hilt modules | Decouples component creation from usage |\n\n### 3.2 Why These Patterns Fit Offline-First Mobile Applications\n\n#### Worker Pattern (via WorkManager)\n- **Problem**: Mobile devices have unpredictable connectivity and power states\n- **Solution**: WorkManager guarantees execution when constraints are met\n- **Benefits**:\n  - Survives process death and device reboots\n  - Respects battery optimization (Doze mode)\n  - Built-in exponential backoff for retries\n  - Constraint-based scheduling (network, battery, storage)\n\n#### Repository Pattern\n- **Problem**: Data comes from multiple sources (local cache, remote API) with different access patterns\n- **Solution**: Single abstraction layer that decides data source based on context\n- **Benefits**:\n  - ViewModels don't need to know about Room or Retrofit\n  - Enables local-first writes for instant UI feedback\n  - Centralizes sync logic and error handling\n  - Facilitates testing via mock repositories\n\n#### Strategy Pattern (in SyncConflictResolver)\n- **Problem**: Different entity types may require different conflict resolution rules\n- **Solution**: Encapsulate resolution algorithms as interchangeable strategies\n- **Benefits**:\n  - Easy to add new resolution policies without modifying existing code\n  - Entity-specific custom strategies via `registerCustomStrategy()`\n  - Default behavior (`LAST_WRITE_WINS`) suitable for most cases\n  - Fail-safe fallback (`SERVER_WINS`) prevents data loss\n\n#### Dependency Injection (Dagger/Hilt)\n- **Problem**: Complex object graphs with many dependencies\n- **Solution**: Declarative module-based dependency specification\n- **Benefits**:\n  - Compile-time verification of dependency graph\n  - Scoped singletons (`@Singleton`) for consistent state\n  - Easy substitution of implementations for testing\n  - Separation of construction logic from business logic\n\n---\n\n## 4. Architectural Justification & Trade-offs\n\n### 4.1 Why This Complex Approach Over Simple 'Fetch-on-Load'?\n\n#### Healthcare Domain Requirements\n1. **Offline Access is Critical**: Patients and clinicians need to record journal entries regardless of connectivity (hospitals, rural areas, airplane mode)\n2. **Data Integrity**: HIPAA-aware applications cannot lose PHI; every entry must eventually reach the server\n3. **User Experience**: Users expect instant feedback; waiting for network round-trips is unacceptable\n\n#### Technical Advantages\n\n| Benefit | Simple Fetch-on-Load | Offline-First Sync |\n|---------|---------------------|-------------------|\n| Offline read | No | Yes (cached data) |\n| Offline write | No | Yes (queued locally) |\n| UI responsiveness | Network-bound | Instant (local-first) |\n| Data loss risk | Connection drops = lost data | Zero (at-least-once delivery) |\n| Background sync | None | Automatic when conditions met |\n\n#### Specific Architectural Benefits\n\n1. **Guaranteed Delivery** (`JournalRepository.java:217-237`)\n   - Entries marked `pendingSync=true` until confirmed by server\n   - Retries handled by WorkManager's exponential backoff\n\n2. **Responsive UI** (`JournalRepository.java:280-284`)\n   - Writes go to local Room database immediately\n   - UI reflects changes without waiting for network\n\n3. **Battery Efficiency** (`SyncWorker.java:218-224`)\n   - Constraints ensure sync only runs when:\n     - Device is on network\n     - Battery is not low\n     - Storage is not low\n\n4. **Conflict Handling** (`SyncConflictResolver.java`)\n   - Explicit strategies prevent silent data loss\n   - User notification for unresolvable conflicts\n\n### 4.2 Trade-offs and Complexities\n\n#### Increased Complexity\n\n| Area | Complexity Added |\n|------|-----------------|\n| Codebase | Multiple layers (Worker, Repository, DAO, API, Resolver) |\n| State management | Track `pendingSync`, `locallyCreated`, `pendingDeletion` flags |\n| Testing | Need integration tests for sync scenarios |\n| Debugging | Harder to trace data flow across components |\n\n#### Battery Usage Concerns\n\n```java\n// From SyncWorker.java - mitigations in place:\n.setRequiresBatteryNotLow(true)  // Don't sync when battery low\n.setBackoffCriteria(BackoffPolicy.EXPONENTIAL, ...)  // Reduce retry frequency\n```\n\n- **Risk**: Periodic background work consumes battery\n- **Mitigation**: WorkManager respects system battery optimization; constraints prevent aggressive syncing\n\n#### Data Consistency Challenges\n\n| Challenge | How Addressed |\n|-----------|--------------|\n| Concurrent edits | `SyncConflictResolver` with LAST_WRITE_WINS |\n| Server timeouts during push | Keep `pendingSync=true`, retry later |\n| Partial sync failures | Three-phase transaction; metadata only updated on full success |\n| Clock skew | Fall back to `revision` number when timestamps equal |\n\n#### Potential Edge Cases\n\n1. **Very Long Offline Periods**: Large queues of pending entries may cause slow sync bursts\n2. **Conflict Notification Fatigue**: Many `MergeConflictException` notifications could overwhelm users\n3. **Data Staleness**: `SYNC_COOLDOWN_WINDOW_MS` (5 minutes) means cached data may be outdated\n\n---\n\n## 5. Key File References\n\n| File | Path | Lines of Interest |\n|------|------|-------------------|\n| SyncWorker | `service/sync/SyncWorker.java` | `doWork()`:88-141, `performSync()`:151-160 |\n| SyncConflictResolver | `service/sync/SyncConflictResolver.java` | `resolve()`:86-143, `preferLastWrite()`:229-248 |\n| JournalRepository | `data/repository/JournalRepository.java` | `performFullSync()`:210-262, `performUpsert()`:268-319 |\n| JournalRepositoryImpl | `data/repository/impl/JournalRepositoryImpl.java` | `trySyncPendingEntries()`:172-199 |\n| DatabaseModule | `core/di/DatabaseModule.java` | `provideAppDatabase()`:74-100 |\n| NetworkModule | `core/di/NetworkModule.java` | `OfflineCacheInterceptor`:152-179 |\n| JournalEntryDao | `data/datasource/local/dao/JournalEntryDao.java` | Sync states:40-55, `upsert()`:170-183 |\n| WellSphereApp | `WellSphereApp.java` | WorkManager init:104-105 |\n\n---\n\n## 6. Conclusion\n\nWellSphere Connect's data synchronization architecture represents a well-designed, production-ready solution for offline-first mobile applications in the healthcare domain. The combination of:\n\n- **WorkManager** for reliable background execution\n- **Repository Pattern** for data source abstraction\n- **Strategy Pattern** for flexible conflict resolution\n- **Dagger/Hilt** for clean dependency management\n\n...provides a robust foundation that balances user experience, data integrity, and system resource efficiency. While the complexity is higher than a simple fetch-on-load approach, the benefits—particularly for HIPAA-aware applications requiring offline access—justify the architectural investment.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Component Responsibilities\n- **SyncWorker** (`service/sync/SyncWorker.java`): WorkManager-based background task that orchestrates sync with network/battery constraints, exponential backoff, and error handling\n- **JournalRepository** (`data/repository/JournalRepository.java` and `impl/JournalRepositoryImpl.java`): Implements the single source of truth pattern, separating local (Room DAO) and remote (Retrofit API) operations\n- **SyncConflictResolver** (`service/sync/SyncConflictResolver.java`): Implements four strategies: `CLIENT_WINS`, `SERVER_WINS`, `LAST_WRITE_WINS`, and `CUSTOM` - with fail-safe fallback to server authority\n- **DatabaseModule & NetworkModule**: Dagger/Hilt modules providing encrypted Room database (SQLCipher for HIPAA compliance), Retrofit configuration with offline caching, and auth token injection\n\n### 3. Architectural Patterns\n- **Worker Pattern**: Encapsulates reliable background execution via WorkManager\n- **Repository Pattern**: Abstracts data sources for offline-first writes\n- **Strategy Pattern**: Enables interchangeable conflict resolution algorithms\n- **Dependency Injection**: Decouples component creation for testability\n\n### 4.
